Its only stated rationale is the hourly cron slot (a run past HH+2:15 makes that fire skip). Derived from it: MANUAL_CHECKPOINT_WATCHDOG_MS + CHECKPOINT_MAX_RUNTIME_SEC RuntimeMaxSec backstop + REPLACE_STALE_MIN_AGE_SEC (release-checkpoint-launch.ts), DEFAULT_REFIRE_RUN_BUDGET_MS 110m, resolveQualificationYieldBudget quantum + TASK_DEADLINE_EPOCH_MS export (green-checkpoint.ts:4550-4554, 11319), the affected-tests deadline-insufficient refusal (scripts/affected-tests.mjs via scripts/lib/task-deadline.mjs + budgeted-task-scheduler.mjs), DEPENDENCY_GENERATION_HARD_TIMEOUT_MS 90m. release-timing-invariants.test.ts pins the chain.\n\nPLAN: (1) remove the run-level wall clock and everything derived from it on the gate path (no RuntimeMaxSec, no deadline export, no deadline-insufficient refusal for gate runs); (2) replace wall-clock wedge/replaceStale with a NO-PROGRESS detector (no test completions + no output + no cgroup CPU for N min); (3) KEEP per-test timeouts (VITEST_UNIT_TIMEOUT_MS etc.) and idle-timeouts, which fail one unit and keep the run going; (4) update release-timing-invariants.test.ts to pin the new invariant (no run-level wall clock on the gate path) instead of the ordering of the old numbers.\n\nWHY NOW: with reuse at 0 (WI-6554700010030941) the full-suite estimate exceeds the 115m budget, so the runner refuses and the gate measures nothing.","kind":"change","title":"Remove run-level wall-clock time limits from the green-checkpoint main-greening path; keep per-test timeouts, replace wall-clock wedge detection with a no-progress detector","summary":"[owner:Avi 2026-10-28 #2574] \"Is there any justification to have these timeouts? The DB dump is supposed to run ~hourly.\n\nTHIS IS THE SIGNAL THAT WAS MISSING. On 2026-07-12 the backup had been failing 100% for ~30 DAYS (newest good dump 2026-06-12) and nothing detected it, because a plausible-looking 1.4GB .gz sat on disk the whole time. 'The backup file exists' is not 'the backup works' — only its FRESHNESS discriminates.\n\nLIKELY CAUSES (check in this order):\n 1. db-backup.sh's timer stopped firing: systemctl --user status papercusp-db-backup.timer papercusp-db-backup.service ; journalctl --user -u papercusp-db-backup.service -n 50. Read /mnt/cold-a/Backup/db-dumps/STATUS.json (ok / failed[] / lastSuccessEpoch) and /mnt/cold-a/Backup/db-dumps/db-backup.log.\n 2. A specific DB's dump is failing validation: db-backup.sh only renames a dump into place after pg_restore -l passes, so a per-DB 'FAIL' in db-backup.log means the archive was unreadable or the dump errored — read the actual pg_dump error there. DO NOT blindly raise PC_BACKUP_TIMEOUT_SEC without reading it.\n 3. An orphaned pg_dump is holding locks (the EI-10730 knock-on): check for a pg_dump backend older than its timeout (SELECT pid, application_name, backend_start FROM pg_stat_activity WHERE application_name LIKE 'pg_dump%'). db-backup.sh tags its own dumps PGAPPNAME=pcbackup and reaps foreign ones; a lingering 'pg_dump%' backend is a legacy/manual dump.\n 4. Disk full on the backup volume: df -h /mnt/cold-a/Backup/db-dumps\n\nAn orphaned/stuck dump is not just a dead backup — it holds AccessShareLock on ~593 tables, which starves every migration's ACCESS EXCLUSIVE and can wedge the whole release pipeline (EI-10730).","kind":"bug","title":"DB backup is STALE: pg/papercusp-transcript is 19h old (>= 6h threshold) — we may have no recoverable database","summary":"EI-10733 durable backup-health probe: '/mnt/cold-a/Backup/db-dumps/pg/papercusp-transcript' has not advanced in 19 hours (size 179B).
